2025-06-17T02:27:18.8582351Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8582554Z <pr_title>
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8583161Z Simplify CI: consolidate bloated workflows and add immediate wheel access
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8583899Z </pr_title>
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8584111Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8584279Z <pr_description>
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8585614Z This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8586951Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8587115Z ## Problem
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8587676Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8588381Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8589320Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8589807Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8590328Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8590804Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8591198Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8591406Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8592442Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8593395Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8593507Z ## Solution
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8593648Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8593765Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8594146Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8594662Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8595095Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8596038Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8596746Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8597072Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8597203Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8597539Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8597750Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8597863Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8598130Z ```bash
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8598910Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8599537Z ```
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8599661Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8599774Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8600056Z ```bash
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8600599Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310...
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8601229Z </pr_description>
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8601380Z

2025-06-17T02:27:18.8772692Z Based on the Tailscale OAuth documentation, here are the customized setup instructions for your GitHub Actions integration:
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8773211Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8773346Z ## Tailscale OAuth Client Setup Instructions
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8773575Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8773688Z ### 1. Create OAuth Client
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8774341Z 1. Go to the [OAuth clients page](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/settings/oauth) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8774928Z 2. Click **Generate OAuth client**
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8775446Z 3. Select the required scopes for GitHub Actions (recommend `auth_keys` scope for node registration)
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8776052Z 4. Add the `tag:ci` tag (or create a custom tag for GitHub Actions)
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8776451Z 5. Click **Generate client**
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8776983Z 6. **Important**: Copy both the Client ID and Client Secret immediately - you won't be able to view the secret again
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8777440Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8777559Z ### 2. Add GitHub Secrets
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8777892Z Add these to your repository's GitHub Actions secrets:
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8778282Z - `TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`: Your OAuth client ID
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8778872Z - `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`: Your OAuth client secret  
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8779131Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8779253Z ### 3. Repository Configuration
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8780308Z The `.github/workflows/copilot-setup-steps.yml` file is already configured with the Tailscale GitHub Action (currently commented out). Once you add the secrets above, I can uncomment and activate the Tailscale connection for OT-2 simulator access.
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8781269Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8781370Z ### 4. Verification
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8782117Z After setup, the workflow will automatically connect to your Tailscale network using the `tag:ci` tag, allowing secure access to your OT-2 simulator for testing Prefect installations.
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8782811Z

2025-06-17T02:27:18.8788337Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8788759Z Based on the Tailscale documentation, here's how to update your ACLs to add the new `tag:ci` tag:
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8789167Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8789273Z ## ACL JSON Configuration
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8789454Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8789693Z Add the tag to your `tagOwners` section in the Tailscale admin console ACL editor:
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8790045Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8790142Z ```json
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8790355Z {
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8790568Z   "tagOwners": {
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8790834Z     "tag:ci": ["your-email@domain.com"]
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8791146Z   },
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8791358Z   "acls": [
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8791581Z     {
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8791808Z       "action": "accept",
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8792079Z       "src": ["tag:ci"],
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8792340Z       "dst": ["*:*"]
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8792584Z     }
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8792801Z   ]
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8793006Z }
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8793216Z ```
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8793461Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8793573Z ## Admin Console Steps
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8793735Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8794044Z 1. Go to [Access Controls](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/acls) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8794610Z 2. Add the `tag:ci` entry to your `tagOwners` section 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8795166Z 3. Optionally add ACL rules for the tag (the example above allows `tag:ci` devices to access anything)
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8795790Z 4. Save the policy file
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8795949Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8796066Z ## OAuth Client Configuration
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8796247Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8796743Z The OAuth client you created should automatically work with any tags defined in your ACL. No additional admin console configuration is needed beyond:
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8797333Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8797572Z 1. Creating the OAuth client with appropriate scopes (`auth_keys` recommended)
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8798090Z 2. Adding the `tag:ci` tag to your ACL policy as shown above
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8798695Z 3. Adding the GitHub secrets (`TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` and `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`)
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8799019Z 
2025-06-17T02:27:18.8799591Z The Tailscale GitHub Action will use the OAuth client to authenticate and automatically apply the `tag:ci` tag to the GitHub Actions runner when it connects to your tailnet.

2025-06-17T02:37:18.3864514Z     39.### Prefect vs Prefect-Client
2025-06-17T02:37:18.3864996Z     40.
2025-06-17T02:37:18.3865847Z     41.For OT-2 and other resource-constrained environments, **use `prefect_client-3.4.6-py3-none-any.whl`** instead of the full Prefect package:
2025-06-17T02:37:18.3866616Z     42.
2025-06-17T02:37:18.3867286Z     43.- **prefect-client**: Minimal installation (~803KB) designed for lightweight environments
2025-06-17T02:37:18.3868087Z     44.- **prefect**: Full installation (~5.8MB) with CLI and server components
2025-06-17T02:37:18.3868749Z     45.
2025-06-17T02:37:18.3869526Z     46.The prefect-client package provides all core workflow functionality while avoiding complex dependencies.
2025-06-17T02:37:18.3870171Z     47.
2025-06-17T02:37:18.3870728Z     48.All wheels are ready for immediate download and installation.
2025-06-17T02:37:18.3871209Z     49.

2025-06-17T02:37:18.3871976Z     51.
